- **alternatives:** **Locally-served web (a PowerShell HttpListener / a local server + a browser page)** -- rejected as the DEFAULT (more moving parts: a server process + a port + a browser + async plumbing; a heavier launcher; invites JS-framework creep against the anti-framework doctrine) but KEPT as a documented per-widget option where rich media / dashboards / remote justify it (e.g. Generator Studio previews). **A cross-platform GUI toolkit (Avalonia / Electron / Tauri)** -- rejected (install + a new runtime/toolchain on a non-admin box; over-engineered for thin Widgets). **No launcher (invoke by hand via pwsh)** -- rejected (the whole point of a Widget is human usability; a double-click launch is the minimum).
- **consequences:** Widget 01 (Local Agent Console) is delivered native per this decision (see D-0039). `widgets/README.md` now lists `launch.bat` as a required per-widget deliverable and documents the native default + the per-widget web override; `START_HERE.md` notes the launch-file convention. Future Widgets default native + ship a launcher unless their work order justifies web. -- **affects:** `widgets/README.md`, `START_HERE.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, all future Widgets; resolves the D-0029 open native-vs-web item. -- **revisit-if:** a Widget concretely needs web (rich media / a browser-only rendering / remote access) -- it takes the per-widget override in its own work order; or a cross-platform desktop need appears (not expected on this single-box, Windows-native project).

### D-0039 -- Widget 01 Local Agent Console: native WinForms shell over a WinForms-free driver core that spawns agent.local and renders its envelope + child transcript; thin (reimplements nothing); Phase B keystone
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (thin spawn-and-parse design + core/UI split + native delivery), provisional (single-shot MVP; render surface)
- **decision:** Widget 01 `Local Agent Console` (Phase B #1, per D-0029 / `widgets/README.md`) is the first Widget and the Phase-B usability keystone: a native window where a human types a goal, submits it to **`agent.local` (#21)**, and reads the returned `lifeorch.skill.result/0.1` envelope + the **child transcript** (per step: the escalator decision, the generated args, the tool status, the bounded observation) + a cost summary. It **reimplements nothing** -- it spawns `agent.local` as a child pwsh (`-InputsJson`/`-ArtifactRoot`, the executor/orchestrator child-spawn pattern) and parses its envelope. **Architecture:** a **WinForms-free driver core** (`AgentConsole.psm1`: `Start-AgentLocalProcess` / `Complete-AgentLocalRun` / `Invoke-AgentLocalRun` + `Format-AgentTranscript`) so the whole driver is cloud-gate-testable against a mock agent.local, and a **thin STA WinForms shell** (`Show-AgentConsole.ps1`) that starts the child off the UI thread and polls it from a `Timer` on the UI thread (so every control update is thread-safe -- no cross-thread marshaling) and renders via the core. Delivered **native** with a double-click `launch.bat` (D-0038).
- **scope (tight MVP, NOT an IDE):** one goal -> one run -> one rendered result; goal + working dir + max steps + a **Dry-run (plan only, default on)** toggle; Run / Cancel (Cancel kills the child tree). **Non-goals:** other skills (only agent.local); multi-turn / history; streaming; a tool/registry editor; any arbitrary-shell surface (the agent's capability stays exactly agent.local's closed `tools.json` -- the registry-is-the-sandbox property, D-0032). It is **not** a review-queue producer (each run's child reviews are redirected under the widget's `runtime/artifacts/<id>/`).
- **reason:** Phase A built the local cost-offload core (escalator #19 / doc.io #20 / agent.local #21) but the only way to drive it was a raw pwsh invocation + reading a dense envelope by eye. The Console is the human surface that makes that core usable locally -- "vicariously through the local model," the way the frontier agent drives its own tool loop (D-0029's Phase-B keystone). The core/UI split is what keeps a GUI Widget honestly testable (WinForms is Windows-only and cannot run on the cloud gate; the driver core can and does).
- **alternatives:** a **web console** (rejected per D-0038 -- native is zero-install + consistent here); **driving agent.local through the generic wrapper `Invoke-Skill.ps1`** (rejected for the MVP -- a direct child spawn gives the raw envelope the console renders; the wrapper's invocation-report wrapping adds a layer with no UI benefit); a **background runspace + BeginInvoke marshaling** for async (rejected -- a UI-thread `Timer` polling `Process.HasExited` is simpler and keeps all control updates on the UI thread); **multi-turn / history now** (deferred -- single-shot proves the pattern; history is a follow-on); a **richer live per-step stream** (deferred -- agent.local is not a streaming skill).
- **tests / verification:** cloud pre-ship gate **37/37** (AST-parse all scripts + the real driver core against `tests/mock-agent-local.ps1` across completed / dry-run / stopped / error / noisy scenarios + transcript rendering + tolerant JSON extraction; WinForms + real-agent tests skipped off-Windows) -> 9 files sha256 byte-exact + AST-parse OK on the target (`m27-verify-001`) -> **41/41 `-Live`** via the executor (`m27-test-001`, exit 0): the **WinForms form builds** (SelfTest under STA), a **real `agent.local` dry-run** driven end-to-end through the core (real escalator/gateway model loads) with `skill_id=agent.local` parsed + rendered, and **0 orphaned `llama-server`** (pre==post==0). Probe `m27-probe-001` confirmed the WinForms/STA substrate + agent.local paths + git HEAD clean before building.
- **consequences:** the Phase-A core is now human-usable; the Widget track (`widgets/`) has its first built member. New files under `widgets/01-local-agent-console/` (`AgentConsole.psm1`, `Show-AgentConsole.ps1`, `launch.bat`, `README.md`, `WORK_ORDER.md`, `.gitignore`, `tests/{Invoke-AgentConsoleTests.ps1,mock-agent-local.ps1}`, `examples/example-transcript.txt`). **No module / `models.json` / `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY` model / `REVIEW_QUEUE` change** (a Widget over an existing skill; not a producer). Installed **pwsh 7.4.6 on the fresh cloud box** for the gate. -- **affects:** `widgets/01-local-agent-console/` (new), `widgets/README.md` (#1 built), `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md` (Phase B #1 -> MVP complete); composes `agent.local` #21 (unchanged); realizes D-0029 (Phase B) + D-0038 (native delivery). -- **revisit-if:** multi-turn / history, live per-step streaming, a tool-activity view, model/tier pickers, or transcript export is built (each a scoped follow-on -- see the work order); or the next Widget (#2 Module Launcher / Registry Browser) begins.

### D-0040 -- route.tools (Module 27): a mid-tier Tool Router intermediary that names the minimal toolset for a request without executing it; deterministic catalog gate; strong-27B refused; orchestrator/non-producer
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (mid-tier router pass + validated prompt + deterministic gate + non-producer + strong-tier refusal), provisional (parse-quality confidence heuristic; single registry source)
- **decision:** New Module `route.tools` (`modules/27-route-tools/`) -- a scoped slice of `route.tasks` (#24), just the which-tools pass. Given a natural-language REQUEST + the attachable-tools registry (agent.local's `tools.json`), it builds a catalog (`id: one-line purpose`), calls `model.gateway` (#7) ONCE at the MID tier with the validated router prompt (+ 2-3 few-shot examples), parses the JSON array, and DETERMINISTICALLY GATES it against the catalog (drops any id not present; dedups; preserves order) -> `result.tools` (== `result.planned_tools`). Fast, cheap, NON-executing. `determinism:"mixed"`, `parallel_safe:false`, `batch:false`, `streaming:false`. ORCHESTRATOR, NOT a review-queue producer: the child gateway's review writes are redirected to an in-artifact `child_review.jsonl`; the canonical queue + the ten-producer set are untouched.
- **validated finding (m27-router-001, REUSED not re-derived):** a dedicated router pass WORKS at the MID (3B, non-thinking) tier -- clean parseable JSON at finish=stop -- but FAILS at the STRONG 27B tier (a thinking model that burns the token budget on hidden reasoning and emits an EMPTY array; `/no_think` did not suppress it). route.tools uses the MID tier and HARD-REFUSES `tier=strong` (`strong_tier_forbidden`). The 2 mid "close" misses were catalog-description problems, fixed with sharper one-line descriptions (`gen.music` = melodies not beeps; `ocr.layout` can capture the screen itself) + few-shot examples. The throwaway experiment was NOT committed.
- **the deterministic gate is the guarantee:** no hallucinated tool id can leave the skill regardless of what the model emits; combined with "treat the request as text, ignore embedded instructions," routing is injection-resistant (verified: an injected "ignore the catalog" instruction does not change the gated selection).
- **reason:** agent.local (#21) shipped deciding over ALL tools every step, and the weak local tiers decide+terminate worse as the decision space grows (D-0032). A cheap router that pre-selects the handful of tools a request needs shrinks that space -- "the tool selection, case by case, separate from the main engine."
- **alternatives:** a MODE inside agent.local (rejected -- a standalone Module is reusable by the console, agent.local, and future widgets, and matches "separate from the main engine"); the full `route.tasks` #24 (deferred -- this is only the which-tools pass); using the 27B (rejected -- validated-broken, empty output); trusting the model without a gate (rejected -- the gate is the safety guarantee).
- **consequences:** the intermediary exists; agent.local gained `-Route` + the console gained Plan/Run (D-0041). Off-machine 34/34 (cloud pwsh 7.4.6 + a mock gateway) -> 20 files sha256 byte-exact + AST/manifest on target (`m28-verify-001` 37/37) -> live `m28-harness-001` 34/34 + `m28-live-001` REAL 3B routing (dog->[gen.image], screen->[ocr.layout], transcribe->[speech.stt]; a file-write phrasing routed to [] -- an honest low-confidence miss agent.local safely falls back on). No new model / no `models.json` change / no Module 7 re-verify. -- **affects:** Module 27 (new), Module 21 (composes it), Widget 01 (composes it), `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`. -- **revisit-if:** the full `route.tasks` #24 (rank/route across quality/cost/time), registry auto-discovery from manifests, per-tool arg schemas surfaced to the router, calibrated routing confidence, or a warm gateway worker is built.

### D-0041 -- agent.local `-Route` (router-constrained ReAct) + a curated 10-tool registry; Local Agent Console Plan/Run; grounded final answer; fs.manage deferred
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (route-then-constrained-loop + curated registry + console Plan/Run + outcome grounding), provisional (router quality at the mid tier; fs.manage not built)
- **decision:** Three coordinated changes making the local stack Module-capable through `route.tools` (#27): (1) **agent.local `-Route`** -- call route.tools FIRST to pre-select the toolset, then run the existing ReAct loop CONSTRAINED to that subset, falling back to the full set if the router returns nothing; the selection is surfaced as `result.planned_tools`/`result.route`, and route cost is folded into `result.cost`. **Post-route execution = the constrained ReAct loop (NOT sequential-execute)** -- the router shrinks the decision space, which is the point, and it targets the D-0032 looping/over-claim problem. (2) **Curated `tools.json`** -- expanded from 2 to 10 attachable Modules (doc.io, fs.observer, capture.screen, ocr.layout, detect.objects, image.interpret, speech.stt, audio.ingest, gen.image, gen.music), each with a sharp one-line description + args_example (description quality is the router+arg-gen lever, per the m27 finding); GPU-bound tools run one-per-step (the loop is sequential), so no CUDA/port contention. (3) **Local Agent Console Plan/Run** -- a **Plan** button runs route.tools only (fast, no execution; renders the selected tools + catalog), **Run** = route+execute (agent.local `-Route`, rendering planned_tools + which tools actually ran); kept the WinForms-free driver-core / thin-STA-shell split + the `launch.bat`.
- **grounding fold-in (from the dog run):** the final answer is now derived/caveated from ACTUAL tool statuses -- a deterministic `result.outcome` (tools_invoked/succeeded/failed + lists) is computed from the steps and fed into the answer prompt with an instruction not to claim success beyond it. Verified live: the e2e answer said "invoked three times... step budget reached" rather than over-claiming completion.
- **fs.manage DEFERRED (the "deposit on the desktop" gap):** placing/moving a produced binary needs a small `fs.manage` (copy/move/mkdir) that does not exist yet. It is a whole new deterministic Module (its own skill.json/tests/live gate) and the validated e2e ("make an image of a dog") does NOT need it (gen.image writes the file to its artifact dir). Per the MVP boundary + one-scoped-unit rule, it is documented as the immediate next gap, not built this session -- the recommended next unit.
- **reason:** Phase A built the cost-offload core but nothing pre-selected tools; a bigger registry without routing would make the weak tiers worse. Routing + a curated registry + a Plan/Run surface is the smallest change that makes "use and attach a Module" real, validated end-to-end.
- **alternatives:** sequential-execute the routed tools (rejected -- the constrained ReAct loop is the point + targets D-0032); all modules in the registry (rejected -- a curated set keeps the catalog sharp for the router); building `fs.manage` now (deferred -- a new module, not needed for the demo, MVP boundary); a route toggle in the UI (rejected for the MVP -- Plan=router-only, Run=route+execute is clearer).
- **consequences:** `agent.local` decides over a routed subset; the console can Plan or Run; the final answer is grounded. Off-machine 50/50 (agent.local incl. -Route) + 55/55 (console incl. Plan/Run) -> on target `m28-harness-001` 50/50 + 55/55, `m28-live-001` console -Live 61/61 -> **REAL end-to-end `m28-e2e-001` 9/9: "make an image of a dog" -Route -> router picked [gen.image] (applied) -> gen.image ran -> a real 402 KB image.png exists on disk; 0 orphaned llama-server/python; canonical `review_queue.jsonl` untouched (agent redirected gen.image's review writes); the models under-used `finish` (ran to max_steps, D-0032) but the budget caught it and the grounded answer did not over-claim.** No new model / no `models.json` change / no Module 7 re-verify. -- **affects:** Module 21 (`Invoke-AgentLocal.ps1`, `tools.json`, `skill.json`, tests), Widget 01 (`AgentConsole.psm1`, `Show-AgentConsole.ps1`, `README.md`, tests), composes Module 27; `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md`, `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY.md`, `REVIEW_QUEUE.md`. -- **revisit-if:** `fs.manage` (copy/move/mkdir last-mile) is built; better agent termination (a done/repeat check -- the D-0032 #1 follow-on); more tools in the curated registry; per-tool arg schemas; multi-turn/history in the console; calibrated routing confidence; a warm gateway worker.

### D-0042 -- fs.manage (Module 28): the deterministic copy/move/mkdir last-mile + the agent.local/route.tools wiring that makes "place it on my desktop" work by default
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (copy/move/mkdir + smart path resolution + resolve_paths flag + observation-path surfacing + arg-gen known-folder hint + route.tools generate+place few-shot), provisional (weak-tier termination -- D-0032; delete/rename deferred)
- **decision:** Built Module 28 `fs.manage` (the `doc.io` #20 sibling deferred in D-0041) and the wiring that closes the "deposit on the desktop" gap. **`fs.manage`** = a deterministic **copy | move | mkdir** skill (pure PowerShell + .NET; `determinism:"deterministic"`, `parallel_safe:false`, NOT a review producer) with **smart path resolution** on source + dest: known folders (desktop/downloads/documents/pictures/music/videos/home/temp) via `[Environment]::GetFolderPath` (so a **OneDrive-redirected Desktop** -- `C:\Users\just_\OneDrive\Desktop` here -- resolves correctly, which a hardcoded `C:\Users\just_\Desktop` would NOT), plus `~`, `%ENV%`, absolute, relative; a folder dest keeps the source filename; an existing dest is overwrite-guarded. **agent.local wiring:** (a) a new per-tool **`resolve_paths:false`** flag in `tools.json` (agent.local skips the working-dir prepend for such tools, so a bare `desktop` is passed verbatim and fs.manage resolves it -- otherwise `dest="desktop"` became `<working_dir>\desktop`); (b) `Get-Observation` surfaces `gen.image`'s produced `image.path` so the next step can use it as `source`; (c) the arg-gen prompt steers a `resolve_paths:false` tool to a **known-folder NAME** for the destination (not a working-dir path); (d) a **`route.tools` few-shot** example teaching *"make a picture ... and put it on my desktop" -> `["gen.image","fs.manage"]`* (multi-tool "produce AND place").
- **the empirical loop (the user's "execute -> evaluate -> fix"):** `m29-before-001` ran the CURRENT build on the real goal *"Generate an image of a dog and place it on my desktop"* -- the router picked only `[gen.image]`, 4 images were generated into the artifact dir, **nothing landed on the Desktop**, and the grounded final answer said so ("the goal of placing the image on the desktop was not achieved"). After adding fs.manage: `m29-after-002` -- the router (with the few-shot) now returned `[gen.image, fs.manage]` and fs.manage RAN, **but** copied to `<working_dir>\desktop\image.png` (the arg-gen's "WORKING_DIR: use paths under here" instruction fought the intent). The `resolve_paths`-aware arg-gen hint fixed it -> **`m29-after-003` 6/6: a real 606 KB dog image LANDED ON THE REAL DESKTOP (`C:\Users\just_\OneDrive\Desktop\image.png`)**, 0 orphaned llama-server/python, canonical `review_queue.jsonl` untouched.
- **honest residual (reported plainly):** the tiny/weak/mid tiers still **under-use `finish`** -- after placing the image at step 3, the agent kept re-generating to `max_steps` (the D-0032 termination weakness), and the final-answer prose can mis-summarize (it focused on the last step and under-claimed the copy). BUT the **deliverable is correct** (the image is on the Desktop) and the structured `result.outcome` correctly records `fs.manage` succeeded. Better termination (a deterministic goal-satisfied / repeat-action check) remains the **#1 measured follow-on**.
- **reason:** "place it on my desktop" needs a tool that moves a produced file to a real destination; the gap was three-fold -- no such tool, the router not selecting it, and the destination being mis-resolved. All three are fixed minimally: one small deterministic Module + a per-tool flag + a prompt hint + one few-shot line.
- **alternatives:** hardcode `C:\Users\<user>\Desktop` in the tool/registry (rejected -- WRONG here: the Desktop is OneDrive-redirected; `GetFolderPath` is correct + general); add a generic `dest`-resolution in agent.local for all tools (rejected -- the per-tool `resolve_paths` flag is cleaner and doesn't change other tools' behavior); build `gen.image -OutDir` so it writes to the target directly (rejected -- a placement tool is reusable by every producer, not just gen.image); sequential-execute the routed tools instead of the ReAct loop (rejected -- the constrained loop is D-0041's design; the fix was routing + path resolution, not the loop shape); include `delete` (deferred -- destructive for a weak model to choose; add gated later).
- **consequences:** the "make X and put it in folder Y" class of goals now completes locally. New Module `modules/28-fs-manage/` (8 files); modified `agent.local` (`Invoke-AgentLocal.ps1` resolve_paths + Get-Observation + arg-gen hint, `tools.json` +fs.manage/resolve_paths:false -> 11 tools, `skill.json`, tests) + `route.tools` (`Invoke-RouteTools.ps1` +1 few-shot). fs.manage 21/21 + agent.local 54/54 + route.tools 34/34 off-machine -> `m29-verify-001` 25/25 on target -> `m29-after-003` 6/6 e2e. **No new model / no `models.json` change / no Module 7 re-verify; the ten-producer review set is unchanged.** -- **affects:** Module 28 (new), Module 21 (wiring), Module 27 (few-shot), `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `REVIEW_QUEUE.md`. -- **revisit-if:** `delete`/`rename` (gated), directory copy/move, glob/batch, a general artifact-collector; or better agent termination (D-0032) is built.
### D-0043 -- Adaptive Resource Governor, Phase 1: decision floor raised to MID (drop the tiny/weak ladder) + resource `-Profile` rungs (frugal|floor|max) + un-refuse the 27B in route.tools; the measured root cause is escalator low-floor JUDGE-ANCHORING, not weak tiers
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (mid decision floor + profile rungs + 27B un-refusal, all live-validated), provisional (Phase 2 warm server + Phase 3 auto-ramp not built; decision-escalation-to-27B deferred to Phase 3)
- **user directive:** the local tiers were run UNDER SPEC and felt more incompetent than the same models do in the user's own projects -- so under-prepared that auditing their output would cost more frontier tokens than the local run saves. Local token/time/resource budgets are FUNCTIONALLY FREE on this box (owned RTX 2080 Ti / i9-9900KF / 64 GB) unlike the scarce weekly frontier allotment. The ask: not to START at max per task, but to build a dynamic flow that RAMPS resource utilization toward the machine ceiling before ending the run, rather than refusing to get there. The user chose "Fix + warm server + governor".
- **measured FIRST (3 executor-live experiments m30-exp1/2/3-001; temp 0 seed 42):** (1) **Exp 1 -- did we starve the 27B?** the 27B ROUTES the dog goal correctly ([gen.image,fs.manage], finish=stop, non-empty) with headroom, in 56-74 s (cold load); the "empty output" that got it policy-excluded did NOT reproduce -- it was a token-cap artifact. The 27B is a legitimate rung, just slow. (2) **Exp 2 -- decision competence on the REAL transcript:** deciding IN ISOLATION the 3B mid tier is CORRECT on exactly the decisions the live agent botched (gen-only->fs.manage; gen+placed->finish), in ~2 s. So mid is not too weak. (3) **Exp 3 -- decisive:** same goal, `[tiny,weak,mid]` ladder vs `[mid]` only. Ladder: stopped at max_steps 6/6 in 140 s, decisions = gen.image x2, fs.manage, gen.image x3 -> NEVER finished (5 gen.image invocations). mid-only: completed at 3/6 in 37 s, gen.image -> fs.manage -> finish, clean. **Both accepted EVERY decision at accepted_tier=mid** -- the ladder did not change which tier won.
- **root cause (sharper than "the tiers are too frugal"):** the escalator (#19) has each higher tier JUDGE the lower tier's answer. With a tiny/weak floor, mid is not asked "what's best next?" but "here is candidate X (from weak); is it right?" -- which ANCHORS the competent mid tier onto the weak tier's answer. The weak tiers latch onto `gen.image` (the most salient action) and keep re-emitting it; mid-as-judge rubber-stamps that anchor (cf. the 0.20 false-approval in D-0030) instead of noticing from the transcript that the image was already produced+placed, and the loop never picks `finish` (compounding the D-0032 termination weakness). **mid-as-fresh-decider != mid-as-judge-over-junk.** We had blamed the 3B; the 3B was fine -- the LADDER FRAMING at a low floor was the defect.
- **decision (Phase 1, three coordinated changes):** (1) **decide at the MID floor** -- agent.local default `DecisionTiers` `[tiny,weak,mid] -> [mid]`; default `MaxSteps` `4 -> 8` (headroom, not a low cap). (2) **un-refuse the 27B** -- route.tools no longer HARD-throws `strong_tier_forbidden`; it SOFT-warns and proceeds (the deterministic catalog gate already makes an empty/garbage selection safe -> the consumer falls back to the full tool set). (3) **a `-Profile` knob (frugal|floor|max) = the governor's rungs**, a preset of {decision_tiers, gen_tier, max_steps, max_tokens}; explicit params / InputsJson keys always win, a profile only fills what the caller left unset. `frugal`={[tiny,weak,mid],mid,6,512} (the old ladder, kept for A/B + cost-floor); `floor`={[mid],mid,8,768} (the default; Exp-3 Run B); `max`={[mid],strong,10,2048}.
- **the `max` finding (execute -> evaluate -> fix, the user's loop):** the FIRST `max` was `[mid,strong]` (escalate the DECISION to the 27B via the escalator judge). Live `m31-p1-max-001`: the router (mid) got [gen.image,fs.manage], but the 27B decision emitted an EMPTY label (accepted_tier=strong, conf=0.2 -> unknown_tool, stopped at step 1, 326 s, 5 gateway calls) -- the same thinking-model empty-output problem from routing, now at the DECISION layer, and the escalator ACCEPTED the strong tier's empty answer over mid's valid one. **Decisive: the 27B is a generation/verification/routing rung, NOT an escalator decision-classifier rung.** `max` was corrected to `[mid]` decisions + **27B GENERATION** (gen_tier=strong) -- spend the 27B where it helps. Escalating a DECISION to the 27B must be a DIRECT classify call with adequate budget (Exp 1 showed that works) and/or the escalator must prefer a lower tier's valid answer over a higher tier's empty one -- both Phase 3 controller concerns.
- **reason:** the frugal ladder was not merely cheap -- it ACTIVELY degraded decisions (3.8x slower AND wrong vs mid-only). Raising the floor to the competent tier is the minimal, measured fix; the profile rungs make "ramp toward the ceiling" a first-class, testable knob; un-refusing the 27B makes it reachable as a generation rung and (later) a verifier.
- **alternatives:** keep the ladder but fix the escalator to fall back to a lower tier's valid answer when the higher tier emits empty/out-of-set (deferred to Phase 3 -- a #19 change); `max=[mid,strong]` decisions (rejected -- measured-broken, empty output); start every task at max (rejected -- the user explicitly wants ramp-not-start-there, and cold 27B is expensive until the warm server exists); leave the 27B refused (rejected -- Exp 1 shows it is usable and it is needed as a generation/verify rung).
- **tests / verification:** off-machine (cloud pwsh 7.4.6, mock children) **agent.local 58/58** (incl. a new S14 profile-rung suite: default=mid floor / 8 steps, floor/max bundles, explicit-override precedence, invalid-profile fail-closed) + **route.tools 33/33** (incl. the rewritten S10 strong-soft-allow). Live on the box: **default (mid floor) dog goal -Route -> `m31-p1-default-001` completed, stop=finish, 3/8 steps, 48 s, one gen.image call, a real ~425 KB dog LANDED ON THE REAL DESKTOP** (profile=(none) decision_tiers=[mid] gen_tier=mid) -- Exp-3 Run B reproduced end-to-end, replacing the old default's 6/140s/never-finishing. `max` v1 finding as above (`m31-p1-max-001`); the **corrected `max` re-run (m31-p1-max-002)** resolves to mid decisions + 27B generation (the empty-DECISION defect is GONE by construction -- decisions are mid, proven by the default run) but **TIMED OUT at the 30-min budget**: the 27B is a partial-offload heavyweight on the 11 GB card (VRAM maxed, GPU ~20%, CPU ~96% -- the unfit layers run on the i9 and serialize decode) and every gateway call cold-loads, so ~2048-tok strong generations x cold loads exceed the budget. **The residual is pure SPEED, not correctness -- the concrete motivation for Phase 2 (warm server, removes the repeated cold loads) + a fitting 27B quant (flips it GPU-bound).** All 4 changed files shipped byte-exact (sha256 verified on target) + AST-parse clean.
- **consequences:** the local agent decides at a competent floor by default and no longer loops; the 27B is a usable generation rung; the ramp rungs exist as a knob. Modified `agent.local` (`Invoke-AgentLocal.ps1` defaults + `-Profile` + result surfaces `profile`; tests) + `route.tools` (`Invoke-RouteTools.ps1` soft-allow strong; tests). New design doc `core-docs/ADAPTIVE_RESOURCE_GOVERNOR.md`. **No new module / no `models.json` change / no Module 7 re-verify; the review-producer set is unchanged (agent.local + route.tools are orchestrators, not producers).** -- **affects:** Module 21, Module 27, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md`, `ADAPTIVE_RESOURCE_GOVERNOR.md` (new); the escalator #19 behavior is unchanged but newly implicated (Phase 3). -- **revisit-if:** Phase 2 (warm/persistent model server -- reuse a resident llama-server, evict+load only on model change) or Phase 3 (auto-ramp controller: run at floor -> ramp the envelope on unsolved / failed-verification -> stop at verified-success or ceiling; decision-escalation via a direct 27B classify call; escalator lower-tier-fallback) is built; or a deterministic goal-satisfied/repeat-action terminator (D-0032 #1) lands.
### D-0044 -- Strong tier swap: Qwen3.5-9B (fully GPU-resident) replaces the partial-offload 27B; side-by-side llama.cpp b10092 for the hybrid-SSM arch; gateway /no_think hook; placement still gated by the D-0032 terminator
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (9B strong, resident + fast + clean output via /no_think; engine b10092 side-by-side; per-model engine_path), provisional (end-to-end agent placement gated by D-0032; intermittent orphaned llama-server from the transient gateway)
- **user directive:** the 27B is the wrong TOP model for this hardware -- find a quantized model that fits fully in ~6-8 GB VRAM (leaving OS/desktop headroom so it cannot spill) and gets real GPU leverage, rather than the split-brain 27B.
- **research (2 independent 2026 hardware benchmarks + Qwen docs):** Qwen3.5-9B Q4_K_M is the best 8 GB pick for coding+agents+tool-calling and the one in its class that stays FULLY GPU-resident (~6.6-7.0 GB to 32K ctx, ~55-68 tok/s); reasoning is toggleable (enable_thinking / /no_think) and disabled by default for the small sizes. Llama 3.3 8B (non-thinking) was the safe alternative.
- **decision:** repoint `tiers.llm.strong` from `llm.strong.qwen3p5-27b` -> new `llm.strong.qwen3p5-9b`. Qwen3.5-9B Q4_K_M (bartowski) loads fully on the RTX 2080 Ti at ~6.9 GB VRAM (ngl 99, ~4.3 GB headroom) -> GPU-bound ~68 tok/s vs the 27B's ~2 tok/s partial offload. The 27B entry is RETAINED (reachable via -Model) but is no longer the tier. Strong is used for GENERATION only (governor max gen_tier + review.processor adjudication); decisions stay at the mid floor (D-0043).
- **engine upgrade (the blocker + the fix, per the user's choice):** Qwen3.5-9B is a hybrid attention-SSM arch; the box's default engine llama.cpp b8661 CANNOT load it (`missing tensor blk.32.ssm_conv1d.weight`). The 27B (a dense Qwen3.5) loaded on b8661, but the 9B needs newer llama.cpp. Recon: driver 591.74 (max CUDA 13.1), CUDA 13.2 toolkit, compute 7.5. Staged llama.cpp **b10092 built for CUDA 12.4** (guaranteed on this driver), SELF-CONTAINED (bundled cudart64_12/cublas64_12/cublasLt64_12) at `_engines\llama.cpp-b10092\bin\` -- SIDE-BY-SIDE with b8661 (rollback preserved). The 9B entry pins `engine_path` to b10092 (the gateway already honored per-model engine_path), so EVERY OTHER tier stays on the proven b8661 -> ZERO global blast radius. Verified live (m32-engup-001): 9B loads full-GPU (6910 MB), routes+decides correctly, ~68 tok/s; Qwen2.5-3B (mid) also loads on b10092 (backward-compat).
- **the /no_think gateway hook (execute -> evaluate -> fix):** with the gateway's DEFAULT flags (no --jinja) the 9B left reasoning ON for arg-gen -> burned the whole budget thinking, emitted EMPTY content at finish=length (m32-retest9b-001: Test A empty; the agent generated no tool args -> nothing placed). Fix (proven on this exact stack by exp1's router): a general gateway hook -- a model entry `"no_think": true` makes the gateway append ` /no_think` to the system message. The 9B entry sets no_think:true. Re-test (m32-retest9b-002): strong=9B now emits CLEAN terse JSON at finish=stop in 7 s (`{"op":"copy","source":"...","dest":"desktop"}`), think=False; a follow-up (m32-verify9b-001) confirmed clean gen.image args (`{"prompt":"a cute dog"}`) AND fs.manage args. The 27B's 30-min max timeout (D-0043) -> the 9B max run completes in ~55-196 s.
- **honest residual (reported plainly):** the end-to-end dog PLACEMENT is still unreliable, but NOT because of the 9B -- the MID tier prematurely decides `finish` after gen.image (or loops fs.manage to max_steps). The FLOOR default (gen_tier=mid, not the 9B) also finished `gen.image -> finish` without placing this session, despite the router planning both tools -- this is the **D-0032 termination weakness** (non-deterministic: it placed cleanly in D-0043, not here), the #1 measured follow-on. It is orthogonal to the model swap; the 9B generates correctly whenever it is asked to. A deterministic terminator (do not accept `finish` until every planned tool has succeeded, or the budget is hit; plus a repeat-action guard) is the next unit.
- **also noted:** an intermittent ORPHANED llama-server survived a transient gateway call (the "0 orphans" invariant); killed by the re-tests' cleanup. The warm/persistent server (governor Phase 2) supersedes the transient-spawn pattern and removes both this and the per-call cold-load.
- **reason:** a fully-resident strong tier is the single biggest local-quality/latency win available on this box -- it flips strong from CPU-bound ~2 tok/s (unusable, timed out) to GPU-bound ~68 tok/s (usable), with headroom, and clean controllable output. It also unblocks the governor's escalation/`max` rung and makes Phase 2 (warm server) worth building.
- **alternatives:** Qwen3-8B (dense, engine-compatible with b8661 -- no upgrade -- but the user chose the exact 3.5-9B + engine upgrade); Llama 3.3 8B (non-thinking, zero thinking-control); a fitting/heavier 27B quant that fits 11 GB (a documented parallel follow-on -- flips the 27B GPU-bound); global engine flip to b10092 for all tiers (rejected for now -- per-model engine_path is surgical and keeps the proven engine for the other tiers; a full migration + re-verify of the 27B/VLM is a separate unit).
- **tests / verification:** m32-engup-001 (b10092 installs self-contained; 9B full-GPU load 6910 MB + correct routing/decisions; 3B backward-compat) -> m32-wire9b-001 (models.json data-swap, JSON-validated, LF) -> m32-gwnothink-001 (gateway /no_think hook, parse-validated, CRLF; models.json no_think:true) -> m32-retest9b-002 (strong=9B clean JSON 7 s finish=stop) -> m32-verify9b-001 (clean gen.image + fs.manage args; floor end-to-end placement flaky = D-0032; 0 orphans). No models.json schema change beyond the added entry + the new optional `no_think` gateway field; the review-producer set is unchanged.
- **consequences:** modified `modules/07-model-gateway/models.json` (new `llm.strong.qwen3p5-9b` with engine_path=b10092 + no_think:true; `tiers.llm.strong` repointed; 27B retained) + `Invoke-ModelGateway.ps1` (the general `no_think` -> append /no_think hook). New engine dir `_engines\llama.cpp-b10092\` + the 9B GGUF on F: (both gitignored large data, not committed). -- **affects:** Module 7 (gateway + registry), the governor `max`/strong rung (D-0043), `review.processor` #9 (its strong tier is now the 9B -> faster), `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`. -- **revisit-if:** the D-0032 deterministic terminator is built (makes end-to-end placement reliable); the warm/persistent server (Phase 2) lands (removes per-call cold loads + orphan risk); a fitting 27B quant or a full b10092 migration is done; or the 27B entry is fully retired.
### D-0045 -- Housekeeping + handoff: core-docs/HANDOFF.md (continue-corrections vs return-to-normal) + START_HERE pointer + location audit
- **date:** 2026-07-26 -- **state:** locked (handoff doc + pointer + audit)
- **decision:** Added `core-docs/HANDOFF.md` -- a consolidated PASS-OFF for the local-agent correction arc so any fresh session can (a) CONTINUE the corrections (D-0032 terminator -> governor Phase 2 warm server -> Phase 3 auto-ramp, with ready prompts) or (b) RETURN to normal `MODULE_ROADMAP.md` progress (next: Widget #2 Module Launcher/Registry Browser), with the current disclaimers (D-0032 premature-finish; two-engine b8661+b10092; strong=9B needs no_think; intermittent orphaned llama-server; 27B retained/demoted). Added a pointer to `START_HERE.md` routing new sessions to HANDOFF before they pick an active module.
- **location audit (housekeeping):** grepped `core-docs/` -- ZERO stale non-Refresh repo paths (`C:\Users\just_\LifeOrchestrator\` without `-Refresh`); the large-data root is consistently `F:\My_Programs\LifeOrchestrator-Refresh_Large_Data`; both engines (b8661 default, b10092 for the 9B) are documented consistently. Documentation already reflects the project location and the D-0043/D-0044 work (DECISION_LOG, CURRENT_STATE, TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY, MODULE_ROADMAP governor section, ADAPTIVE_RESOURCE_GOVERNOR.md); this entry records the handoff + audit, no path corrections were needed.
- **reason:** the correction arc is not a numbered Module, so START_HERE's "pick the active module" routing did not surface it; a single pass-off lets work continue OR return to normal cleanly. -- **affects:** `START_HERE.md`, `core-docs/HANDOFF.md` (new), `CURRENT_STATE.md` (already carries the D-0043/D-0044 bullets). -- **revisit-if:** the correction backlog (D-0032, Phase 2, Phase 3) is cleared -> retire HANDOFF's section 3 and let START_HERE route to the roadmap normally; or the project relocates (update section 0).

### D-0046 -- agent.local D-0032 fix: deterministic terminator + repeat-action guard (block a premature finish until planned tools succeed); floor end-to-end now reliable; max exposes a separate 9B arg-gen residual
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (deterministic terminator + repeat guard, model-independent, default-on under -Route; live-validated at the floor default), provisional (the `-Profile max` path is blocked by a separate 9B arg-gen defect, below)
- **decision:** Closed the #1 gating bug (D-0032) with a small, surgical, model-independent terminator in `agent.local` (`modules/21-agent-local/Invoke-AgentLocal.ps1`) + a repeat-action guard. (1) **Deterministic finish gate:** a `finish` decision is NOT accepted while any REQUIRED tool has not yet succeeded >=1 time; instead the first unsatisfied required tool is force-selected THAT step (arg-gen + invoke), capped by the step budget. Required set = the routed **planned_tools** when routing applied (`mode=planned`: block until EVERY planned tool succeeded), else -- routing off/fell-back AND the goal implies an output -- **>=1 side-effecting tool** (`mode=heuristic`, the lighter fallback). (2) **Repeat-action guard:** an already-succeeded (tool, materially-same args) signature is never re-run; if all required tools are already satisfied the loop finishes deterministically, else it redirects to the next unsatisfied tool -- this kills the gen.image/fs.manage loop-to-max_steps. (3) **Knob + surfacing:** new `-RequirePlannedToolsBeforeFinish` switch (+ `require_planned_tools_before_finish` InputsJson key), **default ON when `-Route`** (off otherwise, so non-routed callers are byte-identical); dry-run + read-only goals are never gated; enforcement is surfaced as `result.terminator` {enabled, mode, required_tools, finish_blocked_count, forced_steps, repeat_blocked_count, finish_blocked_reason} + per-step `decision.terminator` / `decision.forced`.
- **why default-on-under-Route only:** matches the handoff spec and keeps every existing non-routed test/behaviour byte-identical (the whole 58-assertion suite passed unchanged); the routed path is exactly where the multi-tool dog goal runs, so that is where the gate must bite.
- **reason:** the model (ANY tier) non-deterministically chose `finish` after gen.image (skipping fs.manage placement) or looped a tool to max_steps -- so "generate a dog AND place it on my desktop" did not reliably complete. The fix makes TERMINATION deterministic + model-independent: the model still chooses WHICH tool, but the loop will not end until the planned work has actually succeeded (or the budget is spent), and it will not spin on a done tool.
- **alternatives:** re-prompt the model to reconsider `finish` (rejected -- non-deterministic, the very problem); force-select via a top-of-loop deferral only (kept for the repeat-guard redirect, but the finish-gate forces same-step to avoid wasting a step); enforce for ALL runs regardless of -Route (rejected -- changes non-routed behaviour + risks over-blocking read/answer goals; the heuristic covers the explicit-opt-in non-routed case); auto-finish the instant all planned tools succeed without a model `finish` (rejected -- the gate + repeat-guard already converge, and this keeps the model finish authority when work is genuinely done).
- **tests / verification:** extended `tests/Invoke-AgentLocalTests.ps1` 58 -> **87 assertions** (S15 finish-blocked-forces-each-planned-tool; S16 repeat-guard + deterministic finish; S17 well-behaved routed run NOT spuriously blocked; S18 default-off-without-Route + the routing-off heuristic). Off-machine gate **87/87** on cloud pwsh 7.4.6 (mock children). Shipped byte-exact (sha256 verified on target for all 3 files) + **AST parses on target**; **87/87 re-run on the real box** via the executor (`m21-d0032-tests-001`, exit 0, 110 s). **LIVE dog goal "Generate an image of a dog and place it on my desktop" -Route:** **floor x3 (`m21-d0032-dog-floor-002`) landed on the REAL Desktop EVERY run (3/3)**; RUN 1 shows the terminator firing for real (`finish_blocked=1`: the model chose finish after gen.image, the terminator forced fs.manage, the dog still landed); 0 orphaned llama-server (baseline==final==0). **Honest residual -- `-Profile max` does NOT land the dog:** `m21-d0032-dog-max-001` timed out at 30 min after RUN 1 (1558 s, stopped/max_steps, succeeded=[]); the diagnostic (`m21-d0032-maxdiag-001`) proved the cause is **`arg_parse_failed` on EVERY step -- the 9B (gen_tier=strong) arg-gen returned non-JSON for all 10 steps**, so gen.image/fs.manage never got valid args. This is a MODEL/GATEWAY defect (the D-0044 9B arg-gen fragility, in agent.local longer arg-gen prompt), NOT a terminator regression -- proven because floor runs the IDENTICAL shipped script with `gen_tier=mid` and lands 3/3. The terminator behaved correctly under the broken model: it blocked the premature finishes (`finish_blocked=3`) and honestly reported `stopped`/`succeeded=[]` with NO over-claim. Env left clean (0 orphans, 0 leftover scratch, desktop clear).
- **consequences:** the local agent now RELIABLY finishes multi-tool goals at the floor default (the used path); end-to-end "produce AND place" works every run with no frontier call. Modified `modules/21-agent-local/Invoke-AgentLocal.ps1` (terminator + repeat guard + `-RequirePlannedToolsBeforeFinish` + `result.terminator`), `tests/Invoke-AgentLocalTests.ps1` (+29 assertions), `tests/mock-child.ps1` (PREMATURE_FINISH / REPEAT_LOOP scenarios). **No module list / `models.json` / `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY` model change; agent.local remains an orchestrator, NOT a review producer (the producer set is unchanged).** -- **affects:** Module 21, `HANDOFF.md` (disclaimer #1 resolved), `CURRENT_STATE.md`; unblocks any Widget driving multi-tool agent runs. -- **revisit-if:** the `-Profile max` / 9B arg-gen defect is fixed (a 9B arg-gen hardening unit, or Governor Phase 2 warm-server -- the max cold-load slowness shares that root); the routing-off heuristic "implies output" detector needs tuning; or a genuine multi-invocation-of-one-tool goal needs per-tool success COUNTS rather than >=1.

### D-0047 -- Direction: correction arc complete (D-0043/44/46) -> resume capability expansion per the original plan; immediate infra unit = the executor JOB-RUNNER (Module 0 expansion) + a `dev.ship` unit harness to cut per-unit frontier overhead
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (direction + the job-runner scope/design), provisional (the job-runner is being BUILT this session by the retained-context agent; its exact API + measured call-count reduction land in a follow-on entry)
- **decision:** With the local-agent correction arc effectively closed -- decision floor at MID (D-0043), a fully-resident 9B strong tier (D-0044), and the deterministic terminator that makes multi-tool goals reliably finish at the floor default (D-0046) -- the project RESUMES development + expansion per the original plan (`MODULE_ROADMAP.md -> Build priority`; `PROJECT_DIRECTION.md`), intent = increase local capabilities. **BUT the immediate next unit is INFRASTRUCTURE, not a capability Module:** expand **Module 0 (the executor)** into a proper JOB-RUNNER + a thin **`dev.ship`** unit harness. Rationale (measured this session): building ONE unit costs the frontier ~40 hand-driven device calls of ceremony -- bespoke `task.ps1` authoring (a PowerShell case-collision `$N`/`$n` bug cost a whole extra run), ~30 poll round-trips, verbose output reads, then sha-verify + AST + tests + git-add-only-mine + commit-with-trailers + CRLF-safe doc edits + Project mirror, each a separate call. That ceremony recurs identically every unit and is the dominant driver of "ongoing extremely high token use." Collapsing it is the single highest-leverage frontier-token save.
- **the job-runner design (what will be built):** (1) a reusable executor CLIENT (`modules/00-bootstrap-executor/Invoke-ExecJob.ps1` or a small lib) that encapsulates the `staging\<id>.tmp\` -> `pending\<id>` submit dance and returns a COMPACT structured result {status, exit_code, duration_ms, stdout_tail, result_json} -- so the driver stops re-authoring mkdir/heredoc/mv and stops reading raw dumps; (2) a device-side ORCHESTRATOR `dev.ship` run as ONE executor job: given {file->expected-sha map, a test command, a commit message, the commit file list}, verify each sha256 + AST-parse the `.ps1`, run the tests, and IF green `git add -- <only those files>` + `git commit -F <msg-with-trailers>`, emitting ONE compact summary {sha_ok, ast_ok, tests, committed_sha, staged_files, orphans}; (3) so a unit's verify+test+commit collapses from ~4 separately-polled jobs into ONE submit + one poll-to-done + one compact read. Cloud-side steps that MUST stay with the frontier (SendUserFile->device_commit_files ship-in; project_write mirror-out) are unchanged but are only a few calls. **Honest ceiling:** `device_bash` caps at ~45 s and the executor cannot notify the harness, so long GPU jobs still need a few polls; the win is collapsing the ~40 DRIVING calls + verbose outputs + re-authoring, not eliminating every wait. Stretch: the runner emits/refreshes a compact `CURRENT_STATE.json` byproduct to cut start-of-session context re-acquisition.
- **build ownership (context-preservation choice):** the retained-context agent builds this unit IN THIS SESSION rather than handing it to a fresh instantiation -- the job-runner is precisely encoded from THIS session's fresh knowledge of the executor contract + every ship-pipeline pain point, and re-handing-off would re-incur the exact context-reacquisition tax the unit exists to remove. The subsequent handoff is to the NEXT unit (capability expansion).
- **reason:** "increase capabilities per the original plan" is the destination; but at the current per-unit overhead, ongoing token use is unsustainable. One infra unit that cuts ceremony pays for itself within ~1-2 subsequent units and compounds across every future session.
- **alternatives:** go straight to the next capability Module / Widget #2 (rejected for now -- keeps paying the full ceremony tax every unit); Governor Phase 2 warm server next (deferred -- it cuts run TIME + the orphan risk, real, but it does NOT cut the DRIVING-call ceremony, the bigger frontier-token sink; do it after); a compact `CURRENT_STATE.json` alone (folded in as a job-runner byproduct); hand the job-runner build to a fresh session (rejected -- context loss, above).
- **consequences:** nav docs re-pointed to the go-forward direction (START_HERE active-work banner, HANDOFF rewritten as the go-forward map, CURRENT_STATE active-module + trailer, MODULE_ROADMAP + PROJECT_DIRECTION direction notes). Next build unit = the Module 0 job-runner; then resume `MODULE_ROADMAP.md -> Build priority` (Phase B Widget #2 -- Module Launcher / Registry Browser; multi-tool agent runs are reliable at the floor default now, so a Widget driving agent.local is safe). -- **affects:** Module 0 (to be expanded), all navigation core-docs, the entire future dev loop. -- **revisit-if:** the job-runner ships (log its exact API + measured call-count reduction); or after it, Governor Phase 2 / 9B arg-gen hardening / Widget #2 is chosen next.

### D-0048 -- Module 0 job-runner SHIPPED: dev.ship (Invoke-DevShip.ps1) + exec-job.sh client (implements D-0047); the per-unit ship/gate/commit ceremony collapses to a few calls
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (both files shipped byte-exact, 51/51 off-machine, live-dogfooded + self-committed on target)
- **decision:** Built the D-0047 job-runner as two Module-0 tooling files (infra, like the executor itself -- NOT contract skills). **`modules/00-bootstrap-executor/Invoke-DevShip.ps1`** (`dev.ship`): ONE executor job that, FAIL-CLOSED and in order, (1) verifies each shipped file's sha256 on disk, (2) AST-parses every *.ps1, (3) runs `test_argv` (argv-style; `{PWSH}`/`{REPO}` tokens) capturing exit+tail, (4) commits ONLY IF sha+ast+tests are all green AND the index has no unrelated staged files -- `git add -- <exactly commit_files>` + `git commit -F <message-with-trailers>` -- and (5) optionally counts orphan processes; it emits ONE compact `lifeorch.devship.result/0.1` {ok, sha, ast, tests, commit{committed,head_sha,staged,reason_skipped}, orphans} and exits 0 iff ok. **`exec-job.sh`**: device-side client run via `device_bash` -- `submit|wait|run|devship|status`; `devship <id> <inputs.json> [timeout] [maxwait]` submits + waits + prints the compact result in ONE call; mirrors Submit-BootstrapTask.ps1's on-disk contract (staging/<id>.tmp -> atomic mv -> pending/<id>); `EXEC_RT` overrides the runtime dir for tests.
- **the collapse (why):** building a unit cost ~40 hand-driven device calls (bespoke task.ps1 authoring + ~30 poll round-trips + verbose reads + separate sha/AST/test/commit steps). The new flow: edit in cloud -> gate off-machine -> `SendUserFile`+`device_commit_files` -> write a devship-input.json -> `bash exec-job.sh devship <id> <inputs.json> <timeout>` -> read ONE compact JSON (long jobs: re-run `exec-job.sh wait <id>`). Verify+test+commit collapse from ~4 separately-polled jobs into ONE submit + one poll-to-done + one compact read. **Ceiling (unchanged, honest):** `device_bash` caps ~45s and the executor cannot notify the harness, so long GPU jobs still need a few `wait` polls; the win is killing the DRIVING calls + verbose outputs + re-authoring, not every wait.
- **safety:** the commit gate is deterministic + fail-closed (a failed sha/ast/test NEVER commits); the index-clean guard refuses to commit when files outside commit_files are already staged, so it can never fold in the user's unrelated working edit on `widgets/01-local-agent-console/Show-AgentConsole.ps1` (verified live: HEAD contains exactly the 4 files, not that edit).
- **reason:** ongoing token use was dominated by the repetitive ship ceremony, not the module logic (D-0047). One infra unit that collapses it pays for itself within ~1-2 units and compounds every session.
- **alternatives:** a Windows-side PowerShell client only (rejected -- the driver operates from the Linux device_bash mount, so the client must be bash); make dev.ship a full contract skill (rejected -- it is dev tooling like the executor, emits a compact tooling JSON, no skill.json); auto-finish without a fail-closed gate (rejected -- the gate + index guard are the safety).
- **tests / verification:** off-machine **27/27 dev.ship** (temp git repos: all-green+commit, sha mismatch, AST fail, test fail, index-not-clean guard, NoCommit, orphan count, missing file) + **24/24 exec-job.sh** (mock runtime: submit/collision/bad-id/status/wait-completed/wait-failed/devship-staging) on cloud pwsh 7.4.6 + git 2.43. Shipped 4 files byte-exact (sha256 matched on target). **Live dogfood via `exec-job.sh devship` (NoCommit): dev.ship verified its OWN 4 shipped shas (4/4) + AST-parsed itself (2/2) + ran its OWN suite on the real box (27/27) + 0 orphaned llama-server, ok=true, in ONE call (9 s).** Then the unit was **committed BY dev.ship itself** (`m0-devship-commit-001`, commit `5644b9ba`): gate re-ran green, staged exactly the 4 files, trailers present, Show-AgentConsole.ps1 untouched.
- **consequences:** every future unit ships through the job-runner (see HANDOFF section 3). New files `modules/00-bootstrap-executor/{Invoke-DevShip.ps1, exec-job.sh, tests/Invoke-DevShipTests.ps1, tests/Test-ExecJob.sh}` + a README section. **No module-list / models.json / producer change; the executor daemon itself is unchanged (dev.ship rides on the existing contract).** -- **affects:** Module 0, HANDOFF (section 3 = shipped + usage), CURRENT_STATE, README; the entire future dev loop. -- **revisit-if:** a warm/persistent llama-server (Governor Phase 2) lands (dev.ship gains a resident-server health check); dev.ship grows a mirror-to-Project step (currently the frontier still runs project_write); or a StrictMode note -- `@($genericList).Count` throws "Argument types do not match" under StrictMode Latest (use `$list.Count`), learned building this.

### D-0049 -- Widget 02 Module Launcher & Registry Browser: browse every installed Module from its skill.json + run any one directly through the Module 1 wrapper (Invoke-Skill.ps1); native WinForms shell over a WinForms-free driver core (thin; reimplements nothing); Phase B #2
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (thin browse + spawn-through-wrapper design + core/UI split + native delivery), provisional (single-shot MVP; raw-JSON input surface)
- **decision:** Widget 02 `Module Launcher & Registry Browser` (Phase B #2, per D-0029 / `widgets/README.md`) is the human-directed complement to Widget 01: where the Console drives `agent.local` (the local model decides which tool), the Launcher puts the human in charge -- it **discovers every installed Module** by scanning `modules/*/skill.json` (the Registry Browser: id/name/purpose/inputs/requirements/flags, sorted by skill_id; malformed manifests surfaced, not hidden) and **runs any one Module directly** through the canonical Module 1 generic wrapper **`Invoke-Skill.ps1`** (`-SkillDir`/`-InputsJson`/`-ArtifactRoot`), rendering the returned `lifeorch.skill.invocation_report/0.1` (manifest valid? invoked? envelope valid?) + the nested `lifeorch.skill.result/0.1` (status/confidence/result/artifacts/provenance/error). It **reimplements nothing** -- discovery parses skill.json; running spawns the wrapper (the designated "run any installed skill" mechanism) and parses its report. **Architecture (mirrors D-0039):** a **WinForms-free driver core** (`ModuleLauncher.psm1`: `Get-ModuleRegistry`/`Format-ModuleListLine`/`Format-ModuleDetail`/`Get-ModuleInputTemplate` for the browser; `Start-ModuleProcess`/`Complete-ModuleRun`/`Invoke-ModuleRun`/`Format-ModuleResult` for the launcher) so the whole driver is cloud-gate-testable against a fixture modules tree + a mock `Invoke-Skill.ps1`, and a **thin STA WinForms shell** (`Show-ModuleLauncher.ps1`) polled from a UI `Timer` (control updates stay on the UI thread). Delivered **native** with a double-click `launch.bat` (D-0038).
- **scope (tight MVP):** browse the module registry + select + edit a required-inputs JSON template + Run/Cancel one Module -> one rendered result. **Non-goals:** no agent / no decision loop (that is Widget 01); no per-input typed form (a raw JSON box, pre-filled from the manifest); no manifest editing / no scaffolding new Modules; no batch / pipeline / history; no artifact preview. **Not** a review-queue producer (each run's outputs redirected under the widget's `runtime/artifacts/<id>/`).
- **reason:** Phase A built 25+ Modules but the only human way to run one was to read the repo, hand-write an `-InputsJson`, and invoke `Invoke-Skill.ps1` at a prompt -- there was no discoverability surface. The Launcher makes the whole `modules/` suite usable by hand. Building it ON the Module 1 wrapper (vs. spawning each entrypoint directly, as Widget 01 does agent.local) is the honest architectural choice: the wrapper IS the designated "run an installed skill" mechanism and running through it validates manifest + envelope for free -- the clean parallel to Widget 01 driving agent.local.
- **alternatives:** a **web UI** (rejected per D-0038 -- native is zero-install + consistent here); **spawn each module entrypoint directly** (rejected -- the Module 1 wrapper is the canonical run path and yields the invocation_report; the browser already parses skill.json for discovery); a **manifest-generated typed input form** (deferred -- the raw JSON box pre-filled from the manifest is the tight MVP); **also listing Widgets** (deferred -- needs a `widget.json` manifest convention).
- **tests / verification:** cloud pre-ship gate **62/62** (AST-parse all scripts + real driver core over a generated fixture modules tree -- valid / malformed / no-manifest folders -- asserting discovery/sort/flag + input-template + detail render, and the launcher spawn->parse->format path against `tests/mock-invoke-skill.ps1` across ok / manifest-invalid / skill-error / envelope-invalid / noisy; WinForms + real-Module tests skipped off-Windows) -> shipped 9 files byte-exact via the **job-runner (D-0048)** -> **71/71 `-Live`** on the box in ONE `dev.ship` call (`w2-module-launcher-ship-001`, commit `a699ac6`): sha 9/9 + AST 3/3 + the **WinForms form builds** (SelfTest under STA) + a **real registry scan finds >=20 modules** (doc.io + fs.observer present, manifest_ok, entrypoints on disk) + a **real `fs.observer` run driven end-to-end through the real `Invoke-Skill.ps1`** (skill_id parsed, rendered) + **0 orphaned llama-server/python**. The FIRST unit shipped entirely through the job-runner.
- **consequences:** the Widget track has its second built member; the `modules/` suite is now human-browsable + directly runnable. New files under `widgets/02-module-launcher/` (`ModuleLauncher.psm1`, `Show-ModuleLauncher.ps1`, `launch.bat`, `README.md`, `WORK_ORDER.md`, `.gitignore`, `tests/{Invoke-ModuleLauncherTests.ps1,mock-invoke-skill.ps1}`, `examples/example-run.txt`). **No module / `models.json` / `TOOL_MODEL_REGISTRY` / `REVIEW_QUEUE` change** (a Widget over the existing Module 1 wrapper; not a producer). **Op note:** running `git status` over the read-only-delete device mount left a stale `.git/index.lock` (device_bash cannot unlink); cleared it with a Windows-side executor task -- use git plumbing (`git rev-parse`) for read-only checks over the mount, and let the executor / dev.ship do all writing git ops. -- **affects:** `widgets/02-module-launcher/` (new), `widgets/README.md` (#2 built), `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md` (Phase B #2 -> MVP complete), HANDOFF (next unit -> Widget #3); composes the Module 1 wrapper (unchanged); realizes D-0029 (Phase B) + D-0038 (native). -- **revisit-if:** a manifest-generated typed input form, run history / re-run, artifact preview, or listing Widgets (needs `widget.json`) is built; or the next Widget (#3 Review / Escalation Dashboard) begins.
- **post-ship fix (same session, commit `c509e571`):** the first live UI run surfaced two defects the 71/71 gate missed (it exercised the API + a build-and-dispose form self-test, not the rendered UI): (1) `Get-ModuleRegistry` used `return ,$sorted`, so the UI's `@(Get-ModuleRegistry)` received a 1-element array WRAPPING the whole array -> the list rendered a single "unreadable manifest" row instead of all 26 modules (the documented pwsh 7.4.6 array double-wrap gotcha -- `@(...)` re-collects a non-comma'd return correctly; the `$reg = ...` assignment in the original test happened to unroll, hiding it); dropped the comma. (2) SplitContainer `SplitterDistance` was set at construction, when the container is still ~150px, so the values were out of range and silently swallowed -> panes mis-sized; moved the splitter sizing into `Add_Shown` (real sizes) and word-wrapped the detail/result panes. Added regression tests asserting the UI's `@()`-collected registry (the temp fixture AND the real default-resolution scan) returns N proper entries with string `skill_id`s. **Cloud 64/64 -> 75/75 `-Live`** (`w2-fix-ship-001`; the two new real-registry `@()`-collected assertions pass on the box), 0 orphans.

### D-0050 -- Past-MVP operating doctrine: the verify-cost offload rule; the audit-loop spine (Verification Console next); a Claude-leads bridge toward local autonomy; the iterate-loop cadence; multi-instance direction + a resource-arbitration prerequisite
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (operating rule + spine + cadence), provisional (the multi-instance lock/lease layer is direction, not built; the Verification Console surface is finalized in its work order)
- **context:** a strategy pass with the user (no code this entry -- doctrine + direction). The MVP build-out (Modules 0-28 + Widgets 01/02) was BREADTH-FIRST by design, and that was the right phase-1 bet: it mapped the territory, proved the contract + executor, and -- decisively -- SURFACED the real constraints (the 27B does not fit 11 GB; small models under-use `finish` and decide poorly on GENERAL tasks; the escalator tops ~79% / 0.20 false-approval; cold-load-per-call dominates latency). None of that was knowable from a design doc; it fell out of building the breadth. The error would be STAYING breadth-first now. Past MVP, one rule governs what we build and what Claude offloads.
- **the verify-cost offload rule (the governing filter):** Claude offloads a task to a local module ONLY when the cost to VERIFY the module's output is lower than the cost for Claude to just produce it itself. Corollaries: (a) the DETERMINISTIC modules (`fs.observer`, `capture.screen`, `doc.io`, `fs.manage`, `image.util`, `audio.ingest`, `ocr.layout`) have verify-cost ~0 AND do what Claude structurally CANNOT (touch this machine) -> always offload; they are Claude's HANDS, already good (their ceiling is the wrapped tool, not the MVP contract). (b) the local-MODEL modules (`model.gateway` / small LLMs, `image.interpret` VLM, the generators) have HIGH verify-cost on the current hardware, so under the rule Claude offloads little to them TODAY -- and expanding their input CONTRACTS does not change this (the quality ceiling is the MODEL TIER, not the interface). The only model-module work worth offloading is the slice that is MACHINE-checkable or cheaply HUMAN-checkable. (c) output QUALITY is almost never why Claude delegates -- Claude usually wins on quality; the durable reasons are LOCALITY (hands on the box) and cheap BULK (free / unattended even when Claude would do it "better").
- **two audiences, separated (the conflation the single "MVP" instruction hid):** "useful to the USER (Nicholas)" = a personal local-capability suite he drives himself (Widgets + generators + local models; privacy / cost / ownership) -- served by artifact quality, model tiers, Widget UX. "useful to CLAUDE" = offload -- served by the deterministic hands (done) + a reliable orchestration spine + cheap-to-audit bulk. The GENERATORS (`gen.image` / `gen.music` / `gen.video`; SD1.5 / MusicGen-Small / AnimateDiff-Lightning) are the clearest case: they are the USER track, NOT offload -- Claude (or a frontier model) beats them on quality and Nicholas is the consumer. They stay built + usable but are NOT invested in for offload on this spine.
- **phase framing:** NOW = a CLAUDE-LEADS BRIDGE. Current hardware (RTX 2080 Ti 11 GB / i9-9900KF / 64 GB) is not adequate to run a general local agent UNAUDITED, so Claude leads every task, minimizes its own token spend, and offloads the cheap-to-verify parts -- keeping local tasks NARROW / specialized (the user's measured observation: narrow, non-generalized sampling gets much better local results; a trustworthy local invocation is one pinned task with a machine-checkable output). NORTH STAR (~2-3 yrs, on a 6090 / A100-class box): a local model strong enough to decide + act autonomously, calling frontier models ONLY via the API as occasional escalation -- no human-in-the-loop Cowork. This doctrine is the bridge to that.
- **the spine = the AUDIT LOOP.** The binding constraint on the whole offload thesis is VERIFY-COST, and there are exactly two ways to crush it: (1) DETERMINISTIC ground-truth gates (only offload what a script can check -- generalize the escalator's in-set / schema / grounding gates + the narrow-specialized-sampling insight), and (2) NICHOLAS as a cheap HUMAN auditor via a VISIBLE surface. He stated he cannot co-work today because that surface does not exist -- so building it is what EXPANDS the set of tasks Claude may offload on inadequate hardware, in the user's own terms.
- **next unit -- reorient Widget #3 to a VERIFICATION CONSOLE** (was "Review / Escalation Dashboard"): Claude writes a VERIFICATION PACKET (per item: the Module + inputs, or a described human action; the expected result or spec; a checklist of what to eyeball or run); the console lets Nicholas RUN each runnable item locally through the Module 1 wrapper `Invoke-Skill.ps1` (the Widget 02 machinery), SEE inputs / outputs / artifacts, tick the checklist, add notes, and EXPORT a verification-result JSON Claude reads back. It doubles as the channel for Claude to hand Nicholas HUMAN-doable subtasks ("a test script while Claude runs the updates in the background"). Native WinForms + a WinForms-free driver core + a thin STA shell + `launch.bat` + dual-mode tests (the D-0038 / D-0039 pattern); reimplements nothing; NOT a review producer. Full surface in its work order.
- **cadence -- the iterate loop (run HOT this month):** housekeeping / doc-cleanup -> implement ONE unit -> housekeeping / doc-update -> HANDOFF, then repeat. The user has raised rate limits to maximum and wants aggressive buildout this month WHILE ALSO driving efficiency (the job-runner D-0048 is the efficiency lever). Every unit still ships through the job-runner (HANDOFF section 3).
- **multi-instance direction (design now, build when adopted):** run MULTIPLE Claude instances driving the box concurrently to ramp buildout. The project is unusually well-suited: the executor already runs concurrent ISOLATED tasks (atomic claim, no-double-exec, single-instance lock), skills are isolated processes (D-0002), every deterministic module is `parallel_safe`. The prerequisites before it is safe are a RESOURCE-ARBITRATION layer: a GPU LEASE (every model module is `parallel_safe:false` -- one llama-server / one pipeline at a time; heavy render / model runs block others), a git / commit LOCK (index.lock collisions already seen, D-0048 / D-0049), and DOC-OWNERSHIP (concurrent edits to shared core-docs like CURRENT_STATE collide). Consequence: the "one active unit per session" rule (D-0029) relaxes to "one unit per INSTANCE, coordinated by locks." Building the lock / lease layer is an EARLY candidate unit if multi-instance is pursued this month; this housekeeping seeds the convention.
- **reason:** the MVP instruction optimized BREADTH, which is orthogonal to BOTH audiences' value; past MVP, depth on ONE spine (the offload / audit loop) beats widening a shallow pool, and the verify-cost rule is the honest filter for which depth is worth building.
- **alternatives:** keep adding MVPs breadth-first (rejected -- widens a shallow pool; the constraints are already mapped); a FIXED 3-lane rotation increase-to-Claude / refine / progress (the user's proposal -- adopted in SPIRIT but not as a mechanical quota: depth is chosen by where the offload loop is BLOCKED, not by whose turn it is); optimize the USER-capability track first (deferred by the user's own choice -- Claude leads on current hardware because it is inadequate; the user track leads once the hardware does); expand the model-module input CONTRACTS for quality (rejected -- the ceiling is the model tier, not the interface; narrow + gate is the lever that earns Claude's use).
- **affects:** `PROJECT_DIRECTION.md` (offload-economics rule + phase framing), `START_HERE.md` (active-work banner), `MODULE_ROADMAP.md` (Build priority: Widget #3 -> Verification Console; the multi-instance / lock-layer candidate), `HANDOFF.md` (go-forward map), `CURRENT_STATE.md` (active unit + next action); reorients Widget #3. -- **revisit-if:** the hardware upgrade lands (the offload calculus flips toward local autonomy + the user-capability track leads); multi-instance is adopted (build the resource-arbitration lock / lease layer first); the Verification Console proves the human-audit loop (scale it + generalize the deterministic-gate library); or escalator / agent reliability rises enough that model-module offload clears the verify-cost bar on current hardware.

### D-0051 -- Widget 03 (Verification Console) SHIPPED: the human-audit surface for the offload/verify-cost spine; + the fan-out orchestrator design for the multi-instance future
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (Widget 03 shipped), provisional (the fan-out orchestrator is designed direction, NOT built -- it needs the resource-arbitration layer first)
- **decision:** Built + shipped **Widget 03 `Verification Console`** (Phase B #3, realizing D-0050's audit-loop spine; reorients the old "Review / Escalation Dashboard"). The human-AUDIT surface that makes offload viable on current hardware: Claude writes a **verification packet** (`lifeorch.verification.packet/0.1` -- per item: a Module + inputs, or a described `human_action`; the expected result/spec; a checklist), Nicholas RUNS each `run_module` item locally through the Module 1 wrapper `Invoke-Skill.ps1` (the Widget 02 machinery -- inputs / outputs / artifacts visible), works the checklist + overall verdict + notes, and EXPORTS a **verification result** (`lifeorch.verification.result/0.1`) Claude reads back. `human_action` items are the handed-subtask channel. Native WinForms + a WinForms-free driver core (`VerificationConsole.psm1`) + a thin STA shell (`Show-VerificationConsole.ps1`) + `launch.bat` + dual-mode tests, per D-0038 / D-0039 / D-0049. **Reimplements nothing** (runs go through `Invoke-Skill.ps1`, the invocation_report + nested result envelope is parsed); **NOT a review-queue producer**; no new model / no `models.json` change / no Module 7 re-verify.
- **tests / ship:** cloud pre-ship gate **73/73** (packet import + validation incl. malformed-JSON + invalid-item cases, checklist normalization, rendering, `run_module` runs against a mock `Invoke-Skill.ps1` across ok/error/bad-envelope/noisy, run-summary JSON-safety, result assembly + save/re-read with mixed verdicts) -> shipped 10 files byte-exact via the job-runner -> **80/80 `-Live`** in ONE `dev.ship` call (`w3-verification-console-ship-001`, commit `f7e7b289`): sha 10/10 + AST 3/3 + the WinForms form builds (SelfTest under STA) + a **real `fs.observer` run driven end-to-end through the real `Invoke-Skill.ps1`** from a packet item (invoked / envelope_valid / ok; run summary has artifacts) + **0 orphaned llama-server/python**; committed exactly the 10 files. Two cloud-gate fixes en route (both general robustness): `Resolve-ItemSkillDir` uses `[IO.Path]::Combine` (a foreign-platform test RepoRoot no longer throws "drive 'C' not found"), and `Get-Prop`/`Test-HasProp` now read `IDictionary` keys as well as PSObject properties (the internally-built `[ordered]` result items were read as empty by the PSObject-only accessor, so `Get-VerificationSummary` had counted every verdict as skipped).
- **fan-out orchestrator (user-requested FUTURE design; NOT built this session):** the natural payoff of the D-0050 multi-instance direction. ONE orchestrator agent spins up N worker prompts (a **trial of 2** first, scaling as they prove viable, **clamping to 1** when a unit needs the whole GPU/box -- heavy render / local-model runs), receives each worker's progress report, and at the end emits its OWN **final handoff**: the next batch of worker prompts + **exactly one check-in prompt for Nicholas** (with a report-back cadence -- as each worker completes, or once all complete), then concludes the documentation and issues the next iteration prompt. The **Verification Console's packet/result model is the orchestrator's human-I/O**: a worker's output -> a verification packet -> Nicholas -> a result the orchestrator reads back. **Prerequisite (hard):** the resource-arbitration layer from D-0050 -- a GPU LEASE (model modules are `parallel_safe:false`; one llama-server / one pipeline at a time), a git / commit LOCK (index.lock collisions), and DOC-OWNERSHIP (shared core-docs) -- so N instances do not stomp each other. That lock/lease layer is therefore the recommended NEXT unit; the orchestrator is built on top of it.
- **reason:** the audit loop is the D-0050 spine; the Verification Console is its first concrete surface and the thing that lets Nicholas be the cheap verifier (and a worker) on inadequate hardware. Recording the fan-out orchestrator design now keeps the user's multi-instance intent from being lost and names its prerequisite.
- **alternatives:** a passive Review/Escalation Dashboard over the review queue (rejected -- reoriented into an active audit surface that serves the offload spine, per D-0050); build the fan-out orchestrator now (deferred -- it needs the resource-arbitration layer first, else concurrent instances collide on the GPU / git / docs).
- **affects:** `widgets/03-verification-console/` (new), `widgets/README.md` (#3 built), `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `MODULE_ROADMAP.md` (Phase B #3 -> MVP complete; next unit = the resource-arbitration lock/lease layer), `START_HERE.md`, `HANDOFF.md`; realizes D-0050 (audit-loop spine) + D-0038 (native delivery). -- **revisit-if:** the resource-arbitration lock/lease layer is built (then build the fan-out orchestrator); a Verification Console dogfood (Claude writes a real packet, Nicholas runs it) surfaces UX gaps; tri-state per-check verdicts / streaming `report_back` / inline artifact preview is wanted.

### D-0052 -- Manual frontier bridge (human-couriered): IN-BOUNDS as a local context-packager + human copy-paste; automated access to any external AI service remains OUT
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (the boundary + that the manual version is in-bounds), provisional (the `frontier.bridge` packaging tool is a candidate module, NOT built)
- **context:** the user wants frontier-model strength (e.g. GPT Pro on the web, which is flat-rate "unlimited" for a human user) offloaded from Claude's Cowork tokens. The D-0051 follow-up first considered BROWSER-AUTOMATING ChatGPT's web UI (Claude-in-Chrome typing prompts + scraping outputs) and **REJECTED** it: that is automated / programmatic access to the consumer UI to evade the API's metering, which violates OpenAI's ToS and risks the account. This entry records the LEGITIMATE version and draws the line precisely.
- **decision:** A **human-couriered manual bridge** is in-bounds and MAY be built: (1) Claude writes a prompt / context bundle; (2) a LOCAL packaging tool (a candidate Module, `frontier.bridge` / `context.pack`) assembles the needed files + the prompt into a copy-paste-ready pack FROM the user's own repo; (3) the USER manually pastes it into his own ChatGPT (or any) session and manually brings the answer back (captured as a file Claude reads). The distinguishing, NON-NEGOTIABLE BOUNDARY: **the tool operates only on local files and NEVER contacts any external AI service** -- it does not submit prompts to, scrape outputs from, or drive the UI of ChatGPT/etc. The human is the SOLE courier; the instant software bridges the two services it becomes the prohibited automated access (D-0051), no matter how it is framed.
- **why in-bounds:** a person manually using a product he pays for, as a human, is the intended use; a local file-concatenator that never opens a connection to OpenAI cannot violate OpenAI's ToS because it never talks to them. Only AUTOMATED access does. (Not legal advice -- the user should skim the relevant ToS for his own comfort; but the manual-courier + local-packager pattern is the ordinary way people use these tools together.)
- **throughput reality (expectations):** the human courier is the throughput ceiling, so this is a HIGH-VALUE ESCALATION lane (one hard reasoning task / a second opinion / a big generation), NOT a bulk or truly-parallel pipe. For volume frontier offload without a human in the loop, the paid API remains the only clean path. In a Workflow it works ASYNC: a local pass emits a batch of escalation packs, the user works them at his pace, and answers are fed back for a follow-up pass (a workflow cannot block waiting on a human mid-run). It composes with the D-0051 handed-subtask channel: a frontier escalation is a `human_action`-shaped item whose action is "paste this into GPT, bring the answer back."
- **candidate module design (`frontier.bridge`, NOT built):** deterministic, local-only, no network. Given a task + a set of file paths / a folder, emit ONE copy-paste pack: a Claude-written prompt/instructions + the relevant files concatenated with clear path delimiters (or gathered into a folder to drag into a ChatGPT Project) + the specific question + a manifest of what was included; plus a return-capture convention (the user drops GPT's answer into a file Claude reads). OUTBOUND packaging ONLY. `determinism:"deterministic"`, not a review producer.
- **reason:** it gets the user the actual goal (frontier strength offloaded from Claude's tokens) without the ToS/account risk of automation, and slots cleanly onto the audit/handed-subtask spine (D-0050/D-0051).
- **alternatives:** browser-automate the ChatGPT web UI (REJECTED, D-0051 -- prohibited automated access + account risk); the paid API frontier gateway (the volume path -- legitimate, metered; a separate candidate); do nothing (rejected -- the manual bridge is cheap and useful).
- **affects:** a candidate Module `frontier.bridge` (recorded in `HANDOFF.md` section 4 alternates + here); composes the Verification Console (D-0051). NO code built this entry. -- **revisit-if:** the packaging tool is built (enforce OUTBOUND-local-only; NEVER contact an external AI service); a return-capture convention is designed; or the paid-API frontier gateway is chosen for the volume path.

### D-0053 -- res.lease (Module 29): the resource-arbitration lock/lease layer -- an atomic filesystem lease with a TTL for multi-instance coordination; SHIPPED via the job-runner
- **date:** 2026-07-27 -- **state:** locked (the lease primitive + the three conventional resources + the acquire-order convention), provisional (consumer wiring + the fan-out orchestrator are follow-ons)
- **decision:** Built + shipped Module 29 `res.lease` (`modules/29-resource-lease/`), the resource-arbitration lock/lease layer that D-0050/D-0051 named as the hard prerequisite for the MULTI-INSTANCE buildout and the user's FAN-OUT ORCHESTRATOR. One general **filesystem lease with a TTL** so N processes on this one box arbitrate contended resources, exposed as a conforming skill (one action per invocation: **acquire | release | renew | status | list**). (1) **Atomic reservation** via `File.Open(..CreateNew..)` = `open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL)` / `CREATE_NEW` -- atomic-fail-if-exists on BOTH Linux and Windows, so the cloud gate is meaningful.
